# Roadmap — shrike-notify (pub/sub notification brick)
**Maturity:** MEDIUM (publish/subscribe/messages wired). **Stopping point:** a COMPLETE, dogfooded v1. This is INFRASTRUCTURE — do NOT overbuild. Reach v1-complete and freeze to maintenance.

## P1 — complete v1 (next quarter, then STOP)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Tokens endpoint + scoped auth (require_auth path) {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Retention caps + message TTL + topic list/stats endpoints {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Priority/tag validation + quiet-hours delivery gating {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Health details + rate-limit headers + a publish client helper (dogfood) {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
## Stopping point (HARD)
Once the above ships and the fleet + shrike-monitor dogfood it in prod: **v1 COMPLETE — freeze.** No dashboards, no multi-tenant, no fancy routing. It's a notification brick; keep it small and reliable.

## P2/P3 — v1-completion gaps found by code inspection (2026-09-11)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Gate GET /tokens and POST /tokens/revoke behind require_auth — currently in `backend/app/routers/messages.py`, `list_tokens()` and `revoke_token()` have no auth dependency at all (unlike `publish`/`subscribe`, which use `require_topic_and_auth`), so even with NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=1 any unauthenticated caller can GET /tokens and dump every active bearer token, or POST /tokens/revoke to invalidate someone else's token. Add an auth check (e.g. require a valid `scope="*"` token) to both endpoints when settings.require_auth is true, and add a regression test in `backend/tests/test_tokens_api.py` asserting 401 without a wildcard token. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire the already-built TokenStore into token issue/revoke so persistence is consistent with messages — `backend/app/persistence.py` has a fully implemented and unit-tested `TokenStore` (create_token/get_tokens_for_user) but it is never called from `backend/app/services/auth.py` or the NOTIFY_PERSIST startup hook in `backend/app/main.py` (which only wires up MessageStore). Result: with NOTIFY_PERSIST=1, message history survives a restart but the issued-token registry and revoked-token deny-list do not — a revoked token becomes valid again after a restart. Wire TokenStore the same way MessageStore is wired in `startup_check()`, and add a test mirroring `test_broker_with_attached_store_persists_across_a_fresh_broker_instance` for tokens. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Make NOTIFY_MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH actually control the publish body limit — `backend/app/config.py`'s `Settings.max_message_length` (env NOTIFY_MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH, default 4096) and `backend/app/models.py`'s `truncate_body()` helper are both dead code: `PublishRequest.body` in `backend/app/schemas.py` uses a hardcoded `Field(..., max_length=4096)`, so changing the env var has zero effect on what the API accepts. Replace the static Field constraint with a validator that reads `get_settings().max_message_length` (or otherwise wires the setting through), and add a test in `backend/tests/test_schemas.py` that changing NOTIFY_MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH changes the accepted body length. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Delete domain-irrelevant orphaned utility modules with zero call sites — `backend/app/utils/mask_phone.py`, `is_valid_email.py`, `format_bytes.py`, `pluralize.py`, `flatten.py`, `coalesce.py`, `backoff.py`, `dedupe.py`, `topic_slug.py`, and `truncate.py` are each fully unit-tested (matching `backend/tests/test_*.py` files) but have zero call sites anywhere in `app/routers/`, `app/services/`, `app/schemas.py`, `app/models.py`, or `app/main.py` (verified via repo-wide grep) — a phone-masking/email-validation/byte-formatting/pluralization grab-bag unrelated to a topic-based pub/sub notifier. Per the roadmap's "do NOT overbuild ... freeze to maintenance" directive, delete the 10 source files and their matching test files (re-grep immediately before each deletion to confirm no last-minute caller appeared). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove the dead duplicate TTL-expiry implementation before freeze — `backend/app/utils/ttl.py::is_expired(message_created_at: datetime, ttl_seconds)` is a second, differently-signatured implementation of the same check as the actually-wired `backend/app/services/ttl.py::is_expired(msg_ts: float, ttl_s, now_ts)`, which `Broker._purge_expired_and_capped()` in `backend/app/services/broker.py` calls. `backend/tests/test_ttl.py` tests both side by side, but only the `services.ttl` version has a production caller. Two same-named `is_expired` functions with different signatures is a landmine for whoever touches TTL logic after the v1 freeze. Delete `backend/app/utils/ttl.py` and trim `test_ttl.py` down to just the `services.ttl` coverage. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}

## P1/P2/P3/P4 — new gaps found by code inspection (2026-09-14, fleet dry-run refuel)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix phantom `get_token_store` import crashing persisted-auth wiring — `backend/app/dependencies.py::get_auth_manager()` (lines ~91-95) does `from app.persistence import get_token_store` then `await get_token_store(settings.database_url)`, but `backend/app/persistence.py` defines no such function (grep for `def get_token_store` across `app/` returns zero hits — only the `TokenStore` class exists there), so resolving `get_auth_manager` with `settings.persist=True` raises `ImportError` immediately; it's untested (no test in `backend/tests/test_dependencies.py` or elsewhere calls `get_auth_manager`). Add a `get_token_store(database_url)` factory to `backend/app/persistence.py` that constructs and `await`s `.init()` on a `TokenStore` (mirroring how `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` builds `MessageStore`), and add a regression test in `backend/tests/test_dependencies.py` that calls `get_auth_manager(Settings(persist=True))` and asserts it returns a working `AuthManager` instead of raising. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix `is_expired()` signature/type mismatch vs. its only caller — `backend/app/services/ttl.py::is_expired(message_created_at: datetime, ttl_seconds: int)` takes exactly 2 params and a `datetime`, but its only real caller, `backend/app/services/broker.py::Broker._purge_expired_and_capped()`, calls `_is_expired(m.created_at, self.ttl_seconds, now_ts)` — 3 positional args, with `m.created_at` a `float` epoch timestamp (per `backend/app/models.py::Message.created_at: float`). This raises `TypeError: is_expired() takes 2 positional arguments but 3 were given` the moment `Broker.ttl_seconds` is ever > 0; it's currently uncaught because `backend/tests/test_broker.py` has zero tests exercising TTL expiry. Change `is_expired`'s signature to `is_expired(msg_ts: float, ttl_seconds: int, now_ts: float) -> bool` to match the caller, update `backend/tests/test_ttl.py`'s datetime-based assertions to the new float-timestamp shape, and add a test mirroring the exact 3-arg call used in `broker.py`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Close token-revocation authorization gap on POST /tokens/revoke — `backend/app/routers/messages.py::revoke_token()` only depends on `Depends(get_current_user_scoped)` (accepts any valid bearer token, or an unauthenticated `ScopedUser(scope="*")` when `NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=0`), unlike `list_tokens()` in the same file which requires `Depends(require_wildcard_token)` from `backend/app/security/scope.py`. Since `backend/app/services/auth.py::revoke_token(token)` never checks the caller's own scope against the token being revoked, any caller holding a token scoped to one topic can revoke ANY other issued token string (including another caller's wildcard token) just by knowing it. Change `revoke_token()`'s dependency to `Depends(require_wildcard_token)` to match `list_tokens`, and add a regression test in `backend/tests/test_tokens_api.py` asserting a non-wildcard-scoped bearer token gets 401 on `POST /tokens/revoke` when `NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=1`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Delete the duplicate QuietHoursConfig/should_defer_delivery landmine in `backend/app/utils/quiet_hours.py` — this module defines its own `QuietHoursConfig` dataclass (fields: `start`, `end`) plus `should_defer_delivery(dt, config)`/`is_within_quiet_hours(dt, config)`, which duplicate-but-differ from `backend/app/config.py::QuietHoursConfig` (fields: `start`, `end`, `timezone`, used by the actually-wired `backend/app/services/broker.py::should_deliver_now(message, config)`) and from `backend/app/delivery.py::should_defer_delivery(dt, quiet_start, quiet_end)` (separate int args, used by `DeliveryManager.deliver_message`). Two same-named `QuietHoursConfig` classes with different fields and two same-named `should_defer_delivery` functions with incompatible signatures is the exact landmine shape already fixed once for `is_expired` (see the completed TTL dedup item above) — importing the wrong one silently drops the `timezone` field or breaks call sites. Delete `backend/app/utils/quiet_hours.py`'s `QuietHoursConfig` class and its `should_defer_delivery`/`is_within_quiet_hours` wrappers (keep `in_quiet_hours()`, the shared primitive both `app/delivery.py` (line 2 import) and `app/services/broker.py` (line 26 import) actually use), and trim `backend/tests/test_quiet_hours.py` down to just `in_quiet_hours()` coverage plus one new test importing `QuietHoursConfig` from `app.config` to confirm the canonical one still works. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Evict expired/revoked entries from the in-memory `_issued_tokens` registry — `backend/app/services/auth.py::register_token()` adds every issued token to the module-level `_issued_tokens: dict[str, tuple[str, int, int | None]]` dict (line 106) and nothing ever removes an entry: not on expiry, not on `revoke_token()`. `get_active_tokens()` only filters at read time via `is_revoked()`/`expires_at` checks — it never prunes the backing dict, so a long-running process handing out even short-TTL tokens leaks memory indefinitely. This is the same unbounded-growth shape that `backend/app/services/ratelimit.py::RateLimiter._maybe_evict_idle()` already solves for its `_buckets` dict (per that class's own docstring warning about unbounded growth from throwaway keys). Make `revoke_token()` also pop the token from `_issued_tokens`, and have `get_active_tokens()` delete (not just skip) entries whose `expires_at` has passed, then add regression tests in `backend/tests/test_auth.py` asserting `_issued_tokens` shrinks after a token is revoked and after it expires. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Delete 8 garbage tracked files whose names are leftover LLM/aider chat text, not real source — `git ls-files` at the shrike-notify repo root (confirmed via `git ls-files | grep -v '^backend/'`) lists 8 tracked files named things like `Is there a bug in \`delivery.py\`?`, `Is there a bug in \`quiet_hours.py\`?`, `Is there a bug in \`DeliveryManager.deliver_message\`?`, `Actually, the test file has`, `In \`delivery.py`, `In \`quiet_hours.py`, `Let's look at the imports in \`test_delivery.py\` again.`, and `Wait, looking at \`backend/tests/test_delivery.py` — these are aider/LLM response fragments that got written to disk as filenames and committed; none are valid Python module names and none are referenced anywhere in `backend/app` or `backend/tests` (confirmed via grep). Delete all 8 with `git rm` and commit — pure repo hygiene, zero behavior risk. {cat: other; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}

## P2/P3 — new gaps found by code inspection (2026-09-14, refuel pass 2)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Persist and return the validated `severity` field instead of discarding it after validation — `backend/app/schemas.py::PublishRequest.severity` (line 21) is validated via `validate_severity_field()` (lines 101-106, calling `app/utils/severity.py::validate_severity`), but `backend/app/routers/publish.py::publish()` (lines 40-45) never reads `req.severity` when calling `broker.build_message(topic=topic, body=req.body, title=req.title, priority=..., tags=...)`, and `backend/app/models.py::Message` (lines 103-112) has no `severity` field at all — a client-supplied severity is validated then silently thrown away: never stored, never in `MessageOut`/`Message.to_dict()`, never delivered to SSE/WebSocket subscribers (verified via grep — `severity` appears nowhere in `models.py`, `broker.py`, or `publish.py`). Add `severity: str | None = None` to `Message` (and to `MessageRecord`/`MessageStore` in `backend/app/persistence.py` for the persisted path), pass `req.severity` through in `publish.py`, add it to `MessageOut` in `schemas.py`, and add a regression test asserting a published message's severity round-trips through `GET /{topic}/messages`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire `get_auth_manager()`/`AuthManager` into the actual token endpoints so persisted tokens are reachable from the API — `backend/app/dependencies.py::get_auth_manager()` (lines 83-99) builds a `TokenStore` via the now-working `get_token_store()` and returns `AuthManager(token_store=...)`, but `backend/app/services/auth.py::AuthManager` (line 149) has only an `__init__` that stores `self._token_store` — zero methods read or use it — and it's never used as a FastAPI dependency anywhere in `app/routers/` (grep confirms its only callers are its own tests in `test_dependencies.py`/`test_auth.py`). Meanwhile the real endpoints, `backend/app/routers/messages.py::issue_token()` (line 42: `auth.issue_token(req.scope, settings.signing_secret, ttl_seconds=req.ttl_seconds)`) and `revoke_token()` (line 60: `auth.revoke_token(req.token)`), call the module-level functions directly and never pass a `token_store`, even though `services/auth.py::issue_token`/`revoke_token` both accept one (lines 33, 86) — so with `NOTIFY_PERSIST=1`, issued/revoked tokens still never touch the `TokenStore` from the real HTTP API. Give `AuthManager` real `issue`/`revoke`/`get_active` methods that use `self._token_store` when present, have the router endpoints depend on `get_auth_manager` and call through it, and add an integration test that issues+revokes a token with `NOTIFY_PERSIST=1` and asserts the `TokenStore` rows actually change. {cat: backend; size: L; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire quiet-hours delivery gating into the actual publish path — `backend/app/services/broker.py::should_deliver_now(message, config)` (line 172) and `backend/app/delivery.py::DeliveryManager.deliver_message()` are both fully built and unit-tested (`test_broker.py`'s `test_should_deliver_now_*` tests, `test_delivery.py`), but grep across `app/routers/` and `app/main.py` shows neither is ever called from the real request path: `backend/app/routers/publish.py::publish()` (lines 28-47) calls `broker.publish(message)` unconditionally with no quiet-hours check at all, so a low-priority message published at 3am is fanned out to SSE/WebSocket subscribers immediately regardless of any configured quiet hours — the gating logic exists but does nothing in production. Wire `should_deliver_now()` (using `QuietHoursConfig` from `app/config.py`, sourced from new `Settings` fields for quiet-hours start/end/timezone) into `Broker.publish()` or `routers/publish.py` so gated messages are held back from immediate subscriber fan-out per existing test expectations, and add an integration test publishing a low-priority message during configured quiet hours and asserting subscribers do NOT receive it immediately. {cat: backend; size: L; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Expose `min_priority`/`tag` query filters on `GET /{topic}/messages` — `backend/app/services/broker.py::Broker.history()` (line 111) and `backend/app/persistence.py::MessageStore.load()` (line 94) both already implement `min_priority`/`tag` filtering end-to-end (including the persisted-store path), but `backend/app/routers/messages.py::list_messages()` (lines 21-26) only accepts a `limit` query param and calls `broker.history(topic, limit=limit)` — grep of `app/routers/` for `min_priority` returns zero hits, and the only `tag` hits are `publish.py`'s unrelated `parse_tags`/`req.tags`. Add `min_priority: str | None = Query(None)` and `tag: str | None = Query(None)` params to `list_messages()` and pass them through to `broker.history()`, plus a regression test (e.g. in `backend/tests/test_messages_limit.py` or a new file) covering both filters via the actual HTTP endpoint. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Delete the orphaned `delivery_receipt()` helper in `backend/app/services/receipt.py` — this pure function (`delivery_receipt(subscriber_id, msg_id, delivered) -> dict`) is fully unit-tested in `backend/tests/test_receipt.py` but has zero callers anywhere in `app/routers/`, `app/services/broker.py`, or `app/main.py` (confirmed via repo-wide grep — its only matches outside its own file are the import and calls inside `test_receipt.py`); `Broker.publish()` in `backend/app/services/broker.py` only returns aggregate `(delivered, dropped)` counts, never a per-subscriber receipt. This is the same "tested but zero call sites" shape already flagged for the 10 files under `app/utils/` in the prior orphaned-utility cleanup item, just living in `app/services/` instead — that item's explicit file list doesn't cover it. Per the v1-freeze doctrine, delete `backend/app/services/receipt.py` and `backend/tests/test_receipt.py` (re-grep immediately before deleting to confirm no last-minute caller appeared) rather than building new per-subscriber receipt tracking. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Wire rate-limit response headers into the publish endpoint — `backend/app/services/rate_headers.py::build_rate_limit_headers()`/`retry_after()` are fully unit-tested (`test_rate_headers.py`) but have zero callers anywhere in `app/routers/` or `app/main.py` (grep for `build_rate_limit_headers`/`X-RateLimit` outside that one file and its test returns nothing), and `backend/app/services/ratelimit.py::RateLimiter` (line 32) has no method exposing a key's remaining token count — only `allow()` (line 62, mutates and returns bool) and the `bucket_count` property (line 101, total tracked keys, not per-key remaining). As a result, `backend/app/routers/publish.py::publish()` (lines 37-38) raises a bare `429` with no `Retry-After`/`X-RateLimit-*` headers on throttle, and successful publishes expose no remaining-quota signal at all. Add a `remaining(key) -> float` accessor to `RateLimiter`, call `build_rate_limit_headers()` in `publish()` to set headers on both the success and 429 response paths, and add a test asserting `X-RateLimit-Remaining` decreases across successive publishes to the same topic. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

## P1/P2/P3 — new gaps found by code inspection (2026-09-17, fleet dry-run refuel #2)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Wire `settings.quiet_hours_config` onto the process-wide broker singleton at startup — `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` (lines 92-107) reads `settings` and wires `broker.store` when `settings.persist` is true (line 101-104), but never touches `broker.quiet_hours_config` at all (confirmed via `grep -n 'broker.ttl_seconds\|broker.quiet_hours_config\|broker.store =' app/` — only `broker.store =` matches). `backend/app/services/broker.py::Broker.publish()` (line 88) does correctly gate delivery on `self.quiet_hours_config`, and `backend/app/config.py::_load_quiet_hours()` (lines 17-32) correctly parses `NOTIFY_QUIET_HOURS_START`/`NOTIFY_QUIET_HOURS_END` into `Settings.quiet_hours_config` — but nothing ever assigns that parsed value to the live `broker = Broker()` singleton (`backend/app/services/broker.py` line 176) that the real routers use. Setting those env vars in production today has **zero effect**: `broker.quiet_hours_config` stays `None` forever. This is proven by `backend/tests/test_publish_quiet_hours.py` (lines 24-27, 57-60, 79-81), which only exercises the gating by manually doing `broker.quiet_hours_config = quiet_config` inside the test itself — there is no test or code path that goes through `startup_check()` + real env vars. Add `if settings.quiet_hours_config is not None: broker.quiet_hours_config = settings.quiet_hours_config` to `startup_check()`, and add a regression test that sets `NOTIFY_QUIET_HOURS_START`/`END` env vars, runs `startup_check()` (or boots a fresh `TestClient(app)`), and asserts `broker.quiet_hours_config` is populated instead of `None`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Add a message-TTL `Settings` field and wire it into the broker singleton — `backend/app/config.py`'s `Settings` dataclass has no TTL-related field at all (`grep -n 'ttl\|TTL' app/config.py` returns zero hits), and `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` never sets `broker.ttl_seconds` (same grep as the item above confirms this). The process-wide `broker = Broker()` singleton (`backend/app/services/broker.py` line 176) is therefore constructed with the default `ttl_seconds: int = 0` (line 51) and can never be anything else in a real deployment — `Broker._purge_expired_and_capped()` (lines 158-165), which calls the already-fixed `backend/app/services/ttl.py::is_expired()`, only runs its TTL branch `if self.ttl_seconds > 0` (line 162), so it's dead in production; the only place `Broker(ttl_seconds=...)` is ever constructed with a nonzero value is inside test fixtures (e.g. `backend/tests/test_broker.py`). Add a `message_ttl_seconds: int = field(default_factory=lambda: int(os.getenv("NOTIFY_MESSAGE_TTL_SECONDS", "0")))` field to `Settings`, set `broker.ttl_seconds = settings.message_ttl_seconds` in `startup_check()`, and add a regression test asserting that setting `NOTIFY_MESSAGE_TTL_SECONDS` and re-running startup makes `broker.ttl_seconds` reflect it (mirroring the pattern of the quiet-hours wiring test above). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Enforce retention cap and TTL on the persisted message store, not just in-memory history — `backend/app/persistence.py::MessageStore.save()` (lines 82-95) does a bare `INSERT` with no pruning, and `MessageStore.load()` (lines 98-120) only trims the *returned* list via `items[-limit:]` (line 116) when a caller happens to pass a `limit` — it never deletes old rows. Meanwhile `Broker.publish()` (`backend/app/services/broker.py` line 88) calls `self._purge_expired_and_capped(message.topic)` (line 89) before writing, but that method (lines 158-165) only mutates `self._history`, the in-memory deque, and does nothing to `self.store`. Once `NOTIFY_PERSIST=1`, every published message is a permanent row in the `messages` table (`backend/app/persistence.py::MessageRecord`, lines 20-33) — there is no code path, scheduled job, or delete statement anywhere in `app/` that ever prunes it by count or by age (confirmed via `grep -n 'DELETE\|session.delete\|enforce_retention_cap\|is_expired' app/persistence.py`, which only shows the unrelated per-topic `clear()` used by tests). This means `NOTIFY_HISTORY_SIZE` and any future TTL setting (see the P1 item above) silently do NOT bound disk usage when persistence is on — the opposite of what a "retention cap" is supposed to guarantee, and the exact durability gap the roadmap's original "Retention caps + message TTL" P1 item was scoped to close but didn't reach. Add a `MessageStore.prune(topic, keep_max=None, ttl_seconds=None)` method (delete rows outside the cap / older than the cutoff) and call it from `Broker.publish()` after `await self.store.save(message)` when `self.store is not None`, mirroring the in-memory `_purge_expired_and_capped` semantics; add a regression test that publishes more than `history_size` persisted messages and asserts old DB rows are actually gone (row count, not just the read-side slice). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Delete the unverified-signature `validate_scope_token()`/`token_allows()` pair in `backend/app/security/scope.py` — a dead auth footgun — `backend/app/security/scope.py::token_allows(scope, topic)` (line 8) and `validate_scope_token(token, required_scope)` (line 18) are a *second*, differently-shaped implementation living alongside the real, HMAC-verifying `backend/app/services/auth.py::token_allows(token, topic, secret, now=None)` (line 141) that every actual auth dependency uses (`backend/app/dependencies.py` line 55, `backend/app/routers/subscribe.py` line 79). `validate_scope_token()` naively does `token.split(".", 1)[0]` (line 26) and treats that as an already-trusted scope with **no HMAC/signature check at all** — for a real token of the form `<scope>.<issued_at>.<ttl>.<sig>` (per `services/auth.py`'s own docstring), this would accept a totally forged string like `"*.0.0.anything"` as a valid wildcard scope. It is currently harmless only because it's dead code: `grep -rn 'validate_scope_token\|token_allows' app/ tests/` shows its only callers are its own unit tests in `backend/tests/test_scope.py` — no router or dependency ever imports from `app.security.scope`. This is the same "two same-named security-relevant functions with incompatible guarantees" landmine shape already fixed once for `is_expired()` (TTL) and flagged once for `QuietHoursConfig`/`should_defer_delivery` — a future refactor could plausibly wire the wrong `token_allows` into a real auth path and silently disable signature verification. Delete `backend/app/security/scope.py` and `backend/tests/test_scope.py` entirely (re-grep immediately before deleting to confirm no last-minute caller appeared), per the v1-freeze "delete unused security-adjacent scaffolding" doctrine. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
  - **⚠️ CORRECTION (2026-09-19, monitoring/audit session):** this item's "delete the whole file" instruction is now WRONG and must not be executed literally — `backend/app/security/scope.py` also defines `require_wildcard_token()` (added after this item was written), which IS a real, live FastAPI dependency wired into `backend/app/routers/messages.py::list_tokens()` and `revoke_token()` (confirmed via `grep -rn 'require_wildcard_token' backend/app/`) as part of landing the earlier "Close token-revocation authorization gap" item — deleting the file as originally scoped would break token-listing/revocation auth entirely. The dead/dangerous part is still exactly `token_allows(scope, topic)` and `validate_scope_token(token, required_scope)` (still zero non-test callers, confirmed live) — only those two functions (plus their now-orphaned tests in `backend/tests/test_scope.py`, keeping the `require_wildcard_token` tests) should be removed; `require_wildcard_token` and its tests must stay. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Grooming review — 2026-09-18 (dry-since-10:07 batch, reports/grooming-shrike-notify-20260918.md)
Re-evaluated the 04:00 grooming pass's 5 "Proposed" candidates against the live repo. Today saw a large batch of real landed work here (quiet-hours e2e coverage, NOTIFY_HISTORY_SIZE/TTL_SECONDS wiring, retention-cap test, `GET /{topic}/stats`, test refactors), which made most of these stale before they were ever applied. **All 5 dropped — none applied.** No net-new roadmap items resulted; kept for audit trail only:
1. `backend/app/utils/dedupe.py` tests (exact-match, case-insensitive, empty) — exact-match removal and empty-input are already covered by 12 existing tests across `dedupe_keep_order`/`validate_tags`/`dedupe_preserving_order` in `backend/tests/test_dedupe.py`. "Case-insensitive normalization" isn't a real behavior anywhere in `dedupe.py` (all three functions compare items as-is) — the candidate describes a feature that doesn't exist, so there's nothing valid left to test.
2. `GET /health` returning timestamp+version — `backend/app/main.py` already has `GET /health` (returns `status`/`service`/`version`/`uptime_seconds`, plus a real DB ping when `NOTIFY_PERSIST` is on) and `GET /health/detailed`. Functionally already satisfied; not adding a near-duplicate for the literal field name "timestamp".
3. `backend/app/utils/backoff.py` tests (growth/cap/jitter) — `backoff_delay()` and `retry_delay()` are identical exponential-backoff formulas; growth and cap enforcement are already exercised via `retry_delay()` in `backend/tests/test_backoff.py` (incl. a custom-base/cap case). No jitter function exists anywhere in `backoff.py` to test — the candidate again describes a feature that isn't in the code. (Side note, not actioned here: `backoff_delay`/`retry_delay` being literal duplicates, and `dedupe_keep_order`/`dedupe_preserving_order` likewise, look like the same "two same-named/same-shaped implementations" landmine pattern already called out and cleaned up elsewhere on this roadmap — worth a future dedicated look, out of scope for this grooming pass.)
4. `POST /messages/bulk` — genuinely NOT implemented anywhere (grepped `app/routers/`, `app/schemas.py` for "bulk": zero hits); this is the one candidate that isn't stale or already-satisfied. Dropped anyway: this repo's roadmap header is explicit ("INFRASTRUCTURE — do NOT overbuild... It's a notification brick; keep it small and reliable") and the backlog is already deep in P2-P5 wiring/dead-code-cleanup work rather than new surface area. No demonstrated caller/use case for bulk publish exists today. Flagging here rather than adding to the roadmap so a human can make the call on whether it's worth the scope-creep; happy to add it as a P4 `[ready]` item on request.
5. `backend/app/utils/quiet_hours.py` tests (in/out of window, tz edge cases) — already extensively covered by `backend/tests/test_quiet_hours.py` and `test_publish_quiet_hours.py` (added/expanded today), including explicit timezone-offset wrap-around cases (e.g. UTC vs. America/New_York across the quiet-hours boundary). Fully satisfied by today's landed work.

## P1/P2/P3 — new gaps found by code inspection (2026-09-19, backlog-dry refuel — backlog had run to 0 T1-T5 items, every roadmap item was already [decomposed] with none left [ready], so per the refuel doctrine this is a fresh code-grounded audit rather than a re-decompose)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] A revoked token becomes valid again after any process restart, even with `NOTIFY_PERSIST=1` — defeats the entire point of persisted revocation — `backend/app/services/auth.py::verify_token()` (line 61) is the ONLY check `token_allows()`/`get_current_user_scoped()` use to accept a bearer token, and it only ever checks the module-level, in-memory `_revoked_tokens: set[str]` (line 23, `if not token or token in _revoked_tokens`). Nothing anywhere populates `_revoked_tokens` from persisted state: `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` (confirmed via `grep -n '_revoked_tokens\|load_revoked\|revoked' app/main.py` — zero hits) never reloads it on boot, and there is no code path that could, because `backend/app/persistence.py::TokenStore.revoke_token()` (line 203) *deletes the token's row* rather than flagging it revoked — the DB literally has no record that a given token ID was ever issued-then-revoked vs. never-issued-at-all (the two are indistinguishable once the row is gone), so even a correct startup-reload could never reconstruct the deny-list from it. Concretely: issue a token, revoke it via `POST /tokens/revoke` (confirmed via `backend/tests/test_integration_auth_persist.py`, which asserts revocation via "row no longer present" — see that file's own docstring), restart the process, and the exact same token string will pass `verify_token()` again because its HMAC signature is still valid and `_revoked_tokens` is now empty. No existing test exercises revocation surviving a restart (`test_integration_auth_persist.py` revokes and checks the DB row within the same process, never restarts). Fix requires an actual persisted deny-list, not delete-on-revoke: add a `revoked` (or `revoked_at`) column to the `Token` model instead of deleting the row on revoke, add a `TokenStore.get_revoked_token_ids() -> set[str]` method, and have `startup_check()` seed `_revoked_tokens` from it when `settings.persist` is true; add a regression test that revokes a token, tears down and rebuilds the `AuthManager`/`TokenStore`/`_revoked_tokens` state (simulating a restart) against the same DB file, and asserts the token is still rejected. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `app/models.py::Message` declares the `severity` field twice, back to back — lines 112-113 read `severity: str | None = None` followed immediately by `severity: str | None = None` again (confirmed via `grep -n 'severity' app/models.py`, which shows both consecutive lines in the `@dataclass class Message` body). Python's dataclass machinery silently accepts the redeclaration (the second line just overwrites the same class attribute/annotation), so this is currently harmless — `dataclasses.fields(Message)` yields exactly one `severity` field — but it's dead, confusing duplication left over from whichever commit wired `severity` through (likely the "Persist and return the validated `severity` field" P2 item), and a landmine for the next person who edits one copy and not the other assuming there's a reason two exist. Delete the duplicate line; no behavior or test change needed (`backend/tests/test_core.py`/`test_broker.py`'s severity round-trip tests already only see one field either way). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

Delete the function and its test (re-grep immediately before deleting to confirm no last-minute caller appeared). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}

## P1 — Production launch readiness (dogfood-for-real phase, 2026-09-20)

**Why this reopens a "frozen" roadmap:** v1 above is genuinely done — 436 tests passing, tokens/retention/quiet-hours/stats all wired for real (verified 2026-09-20: `GET/POST /tokens`, `GET /{topic}/stats`, `TokenStore` persistence wiring all landed). But until now shrike-notify has been dogfooded only informally. That's changing: the fleet's own `digest_notify.sh`/`deploy_watch.sh`/`daily_promote.sh` already dual-publish to it today (landed 2026-09-18, currently a no-op only because `SHRIKE_NOTIFY_URL`/`SHRIKE_NOTIFY_TOKEN` aren't set anywhere — see `shared/HUMAN_QUEUE.md`), and the operator has said this week they intend to actually provision a real instance and cut over from the public `ntfy.sh` free tier (which just hit its daily quota — a real, current pain point). That raises the bar from "internally tested" to "a real dependency other automation trusts," which surfaces gaps v1 never had to close. Per the infra-brick discipline above, this section is intentionally narrow — production-readiness only, not new product surface — and has its own hard stopping point below.

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Close the token-issuance auth bypass: `POST /tokens` is wide open even when `NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=1` — unlike `GET /tokens` and `POST /tokens/revoke` (both already gated behind `require_wildcard_token`), `issue_token()` in `backend/app/routers/messages.py` has no auth dependency at all, so with auth "on" any caller can still self-mint a `scope="*"` token and fully bypass it. Needs a bootstrap-aware fix (minting the *first* wildcard token can't itself require a wildcard token) — most likely a CLI/one-time-setup path that calls `app/services/auth.py::issue_token()` directly (out-of-band, not over HTTP) for the initial admin token, then gates the HTTP endpoint behind `require_wildcard_token` once `require_auth` is on, with a regression test asserting 401 on an unauthenticated `POST /tokens` when required. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Retry/backoff-hardened Python publish client for real automation callers: `backend/app/utils/publish_client.py`'s `publish_message()` is a single bare `httpx` POST with no retry, and `backend/app/utils/backoff.py`'s exponential-backoff helpers are fully unit-tested but have zero callers anywhere (confirmed via repo-wide grep). Wire them together into a small `publish_with_retry()` (or a `NotifyClient` class) with bounded attempts/timeout and non-raising fire-and-forget behavior on final failure — giving Python callers (e.g. a future gitlark subscriber, or any other in-repo script) the same resilience the shell side already has via `shared/scripts/overnight-queue/shrike_notify_lib.sh`'s `|| true` pattern, instead of a bare unguarded POST. Add tests simulating a transient failure then success, and a final-failure case that doesn't raise. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Pre-cutover smoke-check CLI: extend `backend/scripts/notify_cli.py` (or add a new `backend/scripts/notify_smoke_check.py`) that, given a base URL (+ optional token), verifies in one command: `/health` returns 200, a publish→`GET /{topic}/messages` roundtrip returns the same message back, an SSE subscriber receives a just-published message within a timeout, and — when a token is supplied — that an unauthenticated publish is correctly rejected. This is the concrete tool the operator's planned real cutover needs to confirm a freshly-provisioned instance is actually wired correctly (auth posture, reachability) before pointing `SHRIKE_NOTIFY_URL` at it for real, and doubles as the "load/DoS soak" prerequisite already tracked as a human step in `shared/HUMAN_QUEUE.md`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Cross-repo publish-contract regression test: `shared/scripts/overnight-queue/shrike_notify_lib.sh` (a *different* repo) posts a hardcoded JSON shape (`{"title","body","tags":[...],"priority":"default"}`) to `POST /{topic}` and is the fleet's actual dual-publish caller today. Nothing in this repo's test suite pins that exact contract, so a future change to `backend/app/schemas.py`'s `PublishRequest` (a new required field, a stricter validator, a renamed key) could silently break the fleet's cross-repo integration with no red test anywhere. Add a fixture-backed test in `backend/tests/` that POSTs exactly that payload shape and asserts 200/202, documenting the cross-repo dependency inline so it isn't mistaken for dead-code churn later. {cat: test; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Token minting/rotation helper for automation clients, with the scope tradeoff surfaced explicitly: today a caller must hand-craft a `curl -X POST /tokens` per topic. The fleet's real topic taxonomy is `fleet_<repo>_deploy` (one per repo) + `fleet_queue_task` + `fleet_queue_promote` (see `shrike_notify_lib.sh`), and its current design assumes ONE `SHRIKE_NOTIFY_TOKEN` env var covers all of them — which only works with a `scope="*"` wildcard token, a much bigger blast radius than per-topic scoping. Add a small script/README recipe to mint (and later rotate) either one wildcard token or a full set of per-topic tokens. **[HUMAN] the wildcard-vs-per-topic-scope choice itself is an operator security-posture decision, not something to default silently — this item should present both options clearly, not pick one.** {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}

## Stopping point (HARD, P1 launch-readiness section)

Once the above ships: **production-launch-readiness COMPLETE — freeze again.** These five items close the gap between "internally tested" and "a real dependency other automation trusts" for the specific, real cutover already in motion (ntfy.sh → shrike-notify dual-publish). Do not use this reopening as license to resume general internal polish — this repo's history already shows the failure mode: 875+ commits chasing orphaned/duplicate utility functions with zero callers, most of which have already been cleaned up. Provisioning the actual live instance, setting `NOTIFY_SIGNING_SECRET`/`NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH` for prod, and deciding wildcard-vs-per-topic tokens are tracked as [HUMAN] ops steps in `shared/HUMAN_QUEUE.md`, not fleet work — this repo's job is only to make that swap safe and verifiable once it happens.

## Correction (2026-09-21 refuel — backlog dry again; direct code read against origin/develop @ 8d73d42)
**⚠️ CORRECTION to the "A revoked token becomes valid again after any process restart" P1 item above (2026-09-19 backlog-dry-refuel section) — it is still `[decomposed]`/unstarted-looking but code has since landed that only *appears* to fix it. It does not. Re-flagging as `[ready]` below since the original writeup never actually got worked into a real fix.** Verified live against `origin/develop` (`8d73d42`): `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` now does call `token_store.get_revoked_token_ids()` and seed `auth_module._revoked_tokens` from it (~lines 111-118) — but `backend/app/persistence.py::TokenStore.get_revoked_token_ids()` (line 233) just returns `self._revoked_ids`, an **in-memory `set()` initialized empty in `TokenStore.__init__`** (line 192) that is never read from or written to any DB table or column. `TokenStore.revoke_token()` (line 222) adds to that in-memory set *and* separately still deletes the token's row from the DB (`session.delete(row)`, unchanged from the original bug report) — so a real process restart constructs a brand-new `TokenStore` with `_revoked_ids = set()`, and `get_revoked_token_ids()` on that fresh instance returns an empty set regardless of what was ever revoked before, because nothing durable records the revocation and the underlying row is gone (a revoked vs. never-issued token ID are indistinguishable once deleted). The two tests that look like they cover this both give false confidence: `backend/tests/test_main_startup.py::test_startup_check_loads_revoked_ids` mocks `TokenStore.get_revoked_token_ids` to return a hardcoded `{"token1", "token2"}` rather than exercising a real store; `backend/tests/test_persistence.py::test_get_revoked_token_ids` revokes and re-reads from the *same* `TokenStore` instance within one test, never tearing it down and constructing a fresh one against the same DB file to simulate an actual restart. Concretely reproducible: issue+revoke a token against one `TokenStore(db_url)`, then construct a **second** `TokenStore(db_url)` against the same sqlite file and call `get_revoked_token_ids()` on it — it returns `set()`, and the original token (its HMAC signature still otherwise valid) would pass `verify_token()` again after a real restart.
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Actually persist token revocation so it survives a restart — add a `revoked`/`revoked_at` column to the `Token` model (`backend/app/models.py:128`, currently `id`/`scope`/`created_at` only) instead of deleting the row on revoke; change `TokenStore.revoke_token()` (`backend/app/persistence.py:222`) to `UPDATE`-flag the row rather than `session.delete()`-ing it; change `get_revoked_token_ids()` (line 233) to `SELECT` revoked row IDs from the DB instead of returning the in-memory `_revoked_ids` set. Add a regression test that revokes a token, `close()`s that `TokenStore`, opens a **second, independent** `TokenStore` instance against the same DB file (genuinely simulating a restart, not reusing the same Python object), calls `get_revoked_token_ids()` on the new instance, and asserts the token ID is still present — then confirm `startup_check()`'s existing wiring (already correct, just fed empty data today) makes a freshly-started process actually reject the token post-restart. This is the one remaining gap standing between "internally tested" and the real ntfy.sh→shrike-notify cutover this roadmap's P1 launch-readiness section above is scoped around — a security fix, not new surface area, and not a re-proposal of the still-open "Close the token-issuance auth bypass" item (a different endpoint/vulnerability). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Refuel 2026-09-26 (Claude research pass — fleet backlog-starved)
Roadmap had zero `[ready]` lines (every one of 74 historical features already `[decomposed]`), starving the planner — confirmed via `ovn_planner.log` repeatedly logging "shrike-notify: backlog low (1) but no [ready] roadmap feature". Read `README.md`/`backend/README.md`/`AGENTS.md`, this file's full history (including the already-`[decomposed]` "revoked token valid again after restart" item at the 2026-09-19/21 refuel sections and the already-`[decomposed]` "POST /tokens auth bypass" item at the 2026-09-20/21 sections — both re-verified live and NOT re-proposed here since they already have actionable entries), `OVERNIGHT_DONE.md`, `git log`, and the live `backend/app/` tree read file-by-file. All 6 items below were found fresh on 2026-09-26 and do not duplicate any existing roadmap entry, decomposed or otherwise. Per this roadmap's "INFRASTRUCTURE — do NOT overbuild" doctrine, none add new product surface (no dashboards, no multi-tenant, no new routing concepts) — all close a verified-live gap, a silently-broken documented feature, or a concrete reliability/operability risk.

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Consolidate the duplicate `/tokens` POST+GET routes defined in both `backend/app/routers/tokens.py` and `backend/app/routers/messages.py` — both files independently define `POST /tokens` (issue) and `GET /tokens` (list) with different code, and `backend/app/main.py` includes BOTH routers (`app.include_router(tokens.router)` before `app.include_router(messages.router)`), so FastAPI's routing silently makes `tokens.py`'s copies the only ones actually reachable over HTTP while `messages.py`'s `issue_token()`/`list_tokens()` become dead, unreachable code that still exists and can still pass its own direct-function unit tests. This plausibly explains why this roadmap's long-standing "POST /tokens auth bypass" bug has resisted several previous "fix" attempts (see the 2026-09-19/21 `⚠️ CORRECTION` entries above) — a fix applied to the shadowed `messages.py` copy would look green in its own test while leaving the live `tokens.py` endpoint unpatched. Fix: pick one file to own `/tokens`+`/tokens/revoke` (consolidating `revoke_token()`, which today only lives in `messages.py`, alongside issue/list in `tokens.py`), delete the duplicate from the other file, update `main.py`'s router includes accordingly, and add a regression test asserting the app's route table has no two routers registering the same path+method (mirroring the existing `test_no_duplicate_definitions.py` AST-sweep precedent, but at the router/route level instead of the function-definition level). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Wire `settings.severity_gate_threshold` onto the live broker singleton, and stop duplicating the severity-rank table while there — `backend/app/config.py::Settings.severity_gate_threshold` is a real field parsed from `NOTIFY_SEVERITY_GATE_THRESHOLD` (default `0`), and `backend/app/services/broker.py::Broker.is_actionable()` is fully wired into the real publish path (`Broker.publish()` calls it and drops the message if it returns `False`) — `backend/app/routers/publish.py`'s own endpoint docstring even advertises "Messages are subject to severity-based gating based on server configuration." But `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` never assigns `broker.severity_gate_threshold = settings.severity_gate_threshold` (it only sets `broker.quiet_hours_config`, `broker.ttl_seconds`, and `broker.history_size` — confirmed by direct read), so the process-wide `broker` singleton keeps the constructor default of `0` forever in any real deployment, making `NOTIFY_SEVERITY_GATE_THRESHOLD` a complete no-op despite being documented as a real feature — the exact "parsed into Settings but never reaches the broker singleton" shape already fixed once for quiet-hours (2026-09-17 roadmap item) and once for TTL, recurring a third time for severity. Separately, `Broker.is_actionable()` reimplements its own hardcoded `severity_map` dict (`debug/info/warning/error/critical` → `0-4`) instead of calling the already-tested canonical `backend/app/utils/severity.py::severity_rank()`/`SEVERITY_RANK_MAP` — the same "two same-shaped implementations, only one wired" duplication pattern already fixed for TTL, quiet-hours, and dedupe elsewhere on this roadmap. Fix both: set `broker.severity_gate_threshold = settings.severity_gate_threshold` in `startup_check()`, and have `Broker.is_actionable()` delegate to `app.utils.severity.severity_rank()` instead of its inline copy. Add a regression test setting `NOTIFY_SEVERITY_GATE_THRESHOLD` and asserting a fresh `startup_check()` run makes `broker.severity_gate_threshold` reflect it (mirroring the existing quiet-hours-wiring test pattern), plus an integration test publishing a low-severity message with gating configured end-to-end and asserting it's actually suppressed. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Close a live, currently-unmatched blind spot in the garbage-filename pre-commit guard — `backend/scripts/cleanup_garbage.py::GARBAGE_PATTERNS` is the exact tool built (2026-09-14/17) to catch aider/LLM chat-text fragments that get committed as filenames, and `.pre-commit-config.yaml` runs it as a hook on every commit, but a NEW instance has slipped past it: a tracked file literally named `Looking at the broker initialization` (containing one line, `broker = Broker()`) exists in the repo root today and matches none of the 7 current `GARBAGE_PATTERNS` entries (confirmed via direct `git ls-files` + `cat` + grep against the pattern list on 2026-09-26). This is the identical failure mode `cleanup_garbage.py`'s own docstring already documents happening twice before (a 7th missing pattern, then an unwalked garbage-named directory) — a live third instance, not a hypothetical one. Add `"Looking at the broker"` (or a broader heuristic matching filenames starting with a capital letter and containing spaces/backticks, which no real Python module name would) to `GARBAGE_PATTERNS`, delete the file with `git rm`, and re-run `cleanup_garbage.py --check` to confirm no further instances remain. {cat: other; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Run the production Docker container as a non-root user — `backend/Dockerfile` (`FROM python:3.12-slim`, `WORKDIR /app`, installs deps, `COPY . .`, `CMD ["./start.sh"]`) has no `USER` directive anywhere, so the `uvicorn` process serving real production traffic runs as root inside the container — standard container-hardening practice that costs one `RUN useradd` + one `USER` line and doesn't touch app behavior at all. This matters concretely now, not hypothetically: the roadmap's own "Production launch readiness" section (2026-09-20) documents an active, real cutover from the public `ntfy.sh` free tier to a self-hosted shrike-notify instance — exactly the point at which "runs as root in prod" stops being a dev-only detail. Add a non-root user in the Dockerfile (`RUN useradd -m appuser && chown -R appuser /app` + `USER appuser` before `CMD`), verify the image still builds and `start.sh` still runs (no write-permission assumptions broken — check whether `NOTIFY_DATABASE_URL`'s default sqlite file path needs a writable directory owned by the new user), and note the change in `backend/README.md`'s deployment section if one exists. {cat: other; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Pin exact dependency versions in `backend/requirements.txt` instead of unbounded floors — every line in the file today is a bare `package>=X.Y.Z` (`fastapi>=0.115.0`, `pydantic>=2.7.0`, `sqlalchemy>=2.0.0`, `asyncpg>=0.29.0`, etc., confirmed via direct read on 2026-09-26) with no upper bound and no lock file anywhere in the repo, and `backend/Dockerfile` does a bare `pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt` on every image build — meaning two production builds a week apart, with no code change at all, can silently resolve to different (potentially breaking-major-version) dependency versions. Pin every entry to the exact version currently tested against (`==` instead of `>=`, taken from the versions already installed in the working `.venv`/CI environment), so a rebuild is reproducible; a deliberate version bump then becomes an explicit, reviewable diff instead of an invisible floor-drift. Verify `cd backend && pip install -r requirements.txt && python -m pytest -q --no-cov` still passes fully pinned. {cat: other; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Add backup/editor-artifact patterns to `.gitignore` — `.gitignore` today covers `.venv/`, `__pycache__/`, `*.pyc`, `.pytest_cache/`, `.env`, `*.db`, `.DS_Store`, `.aider*`, `.coverage`, `htmlcov/`, but nothing matches a plain `*.bak` — and a real `OVERNIGHT_PROGRESS.md.bak` file is sitting untracked in the repo's working tree right now (confirmed via `git status` on 2026-09-26), one broad `git add -A`/`git add .` away from being accidentally committed as tracked cruft, the same class of repo-hygiene problem the garbage-filename item above addresses for a different cause. Add `*.bak`, `*.orig`, and `*.swp` to `.gitignore`; no code change needed. {cat: other; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Research refuel 2026-09-30 (interactive Claude research pass; 6 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Gate message-history, stats and topic-list reads behind auth when NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=1 — `backend/app/routers/messages.py::list_messages()` and `get_stats()` depend only on `require_valid_topic` (format check, no token check), and `backend/app/routers/publish.py::list_topics()` has no dependency at all, whereas publish and `/{topic}/sse` use `require_topic_and_auth`. With auth on, anyone can GET /{topic}/messages and read full retained message bodies (fleet deploy alerts etc.) and enumerate all topic names via GET /topics. Switch the two history routes to `require_topic_and_auth` and make /topics require a wildcard token via `require_wildcard_token` (app/security/scope.py). Add 401/200 tests with require_auth patched on. Distinct from the tokens-endpoint items. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Refuse to start when NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=1 and the signing secret is the default — `backend/app/main.py::startup_check()` only logs a warning when `secret_is_insecure(settings)` (app/config.py, default "dev-insecure-secret" is public in the repo), so a Railway deploy that forgets NOTIFY_SIGNING_SECRET runs "authenticated" while anyone can forge valid `*` tokens by computing the HMAC in app/services/auth.py::_sign. Raise RuntimeError at startup in that combination (allow an explicit NOTIFY_ALLOW_INSECURE_SECRET=1 escape hatch for tests/dev); update test_main_startup.py to assert the failure and the override. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Apply message TTL at read time, not only on the next publish — TTL pruning runs only inside `Broker.publish()` (`_purge_expired_and_capped`, `store.prune`). `Broker.history()` (in-memory branch) and `persistence.py::MessageStore.load()` never filter by `ttl_seconds`, so on a quiet topic expired messages are still returned by GET /{topic}/messages, /stats, and replayed to every new SSE/WS subscriber in `app/routers/subscribe.py` until someone publishes again (default TTL is 3600s via config.message_ttl_seconds). Filter with `app/services/ttl.py::is_expired` in both history paths (create_at is a float epoch) and add tests with a stale message and no subsequent publish. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Make publish resilient to a failing message store — in `backend/app/services/broker.py::Broker.publish()` the message is appended to in-memory history, then `await self.store.save(message)` and `store.prune(...)` run with no try/except BEFORE fan-out to subscriber queues. A transient DB error (Postgres blip, SQLite lock) raises out of the handler: POST returns 500 and live SSE/WS subscribers never receive a message that is already in memory history, while the publisher's retry then duplicates it. Wrap save/prune in try/except that logs the error, still fans out, and surfaces persistence failure (e.g. a `persisted: false` field or log counter). Test with a store stub whose save() raises. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Stop fire-and-forget token persistence from silently losing writes — `backend/app/services/auth.py::issue_token()` and `revoke_token()` call `loop.create_task(token_store.create_token/revoke_token(...))` without keeping a reference or a done-callback, and `AuthManager.revoke()` does the same. Un-referenced tasks can be garbage-collected mid-flight and their exceptions are swallowed, yet POST /tokens returns the token and POST /tokens/revoke returns 204 regardless; `revoke_token()` with no running loop (`except RuntimeError: pass`) skips persistence entirely. Keep tasks in a module-level set with `add_done_callback` that discards and logs exceptions at ERROR level, and add a test where the store raises and the error is logged. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Give SSE frames an `id:` and honor `Last-Event-ID` so reconnects don't replay everything — `backend/app/routers/subscribe.py::format_sse()` emits only `data: ...` and `subscribe()` replays the entire `history_size` window on every connect, so any EventSource reconnect (dropped network, proxy idle cut) re-delivers up to 100 already-seen alerts to the consumer. Emit `id: <message.id>` per frame and, when a `Last-Event-ID` header is present, replay only messages after that id in history (fall back to full replay if the id is unknown). Add tests in test_subscribe_api.py. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-01 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 7 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Invalid `NOTIFY_QUIET_HOURS_TZ` string causes uncaught `ValueError` in `broker.publish()` — `backend/app/config.py:52` stores any `NOTIFY_QUIET_HOURS_TZ` value as-is with no IANA validation; `backend/app/utils/quiet_hours.py:95` calls `resolve_timezone(getattr(config, "timezone", "UTC"))` which raises `ValueError` for unrecognized zone names (via `ZoneInfoNotFoundError`); `backend/app/services/broker.py:114-116` calls `should_deliver_now()` inside a bare `if self.quiet_hours_config is not None:` with no try/except, so a single misconfigured env var makes every `POST /{topic}` return 500. Fix: call `resolve_timezone(tz)` inside `_load_quiet_hours()`, catch `ValueError`, log a warning and fall back to UTC; add a regression test that a bad TZ string produces a working `Settings` and publish succeeds. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `startup_check()` creates a `TokenStore` engine that's never closed, leaking DB connections — `backend/app/main.py:112-117` creates `token_store = TokenStore(settings.database_url)` as a local variable used only to seed `_revoked_tokens`; `shutdown_cleanup():128-132` only calls `broker.store.close()`, ignoring this engine and `backend/app/dependencies.py`'s module-global `_token_store_instance` (also never disposed). With PostgreSQL, each unreleased SQLAlchemy async engine holds an open pool; repeated container restarts exhaust the DB's `max_connections`. Fix: store the startup `TokenStore` in `app.state`, close both it and `_token_store_instance` in `shutdown_cleanup()`; add a test asserting `shutdown_cleanup` disposes both. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `backend/tests/test_tokens_api.py` is missing despite 5+ roadmap items referencing it as the target for landed fixes — `backend/app/routers/tokens.py` registers `POST /tokens`, `GET /tokens`, and `POST /tokens/revoke` with bootstrap gating via `require_wildcard_or_bootstrap` (`backend/app/security/scope.py:57-83`), but no HTTP-level test file for these routes exists in `backend/tests/`. Roadmap entries from 2026-09-20/21 reference `test_post_tokens_401_unauth_once_bootstrapped` and `test_post_tokens_200_when_no_wildcard_exists_yet` as VERIFY targets, but `ls backend/tests/` confirms neither the file nor the tests exist. Add `test_tokens_api.py` covering: unauthenticated `POST /tokens` returns 200 pre-bootstrap; 401 once a wildcard token exists; `GET /tokens` returns the issued token; `POST /tokens/revoke` removes it; 422 for invalid scope. {cat: test; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Hardcoded `allow_origins=["*"]` CORS with no environment override — `backend/app/main.py:43-48` unconditionally applies `CORSMiddleware(allow_origins=["*"], allow_methods=["*"], allow_headers=["*"])`; `backend/app/config.py` has no `NOTIFY_CORS_ORIGINS` field. With `NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=1`, any website can make cross-origin browser GET requests to `/{topic}/messages` and read retained notification bodies (fleet deploy alerts, etc.) without the bearer token being a practical CORS barrier. Add `cors_origins: list[str]` to `Settings` (env `NOTIFY_CORS_ORIGINS`, comma-separated, default `["*"]`), wire into `CORSMiddleware(allow_origins=settings.cors_origins)`, add a test that setting the env var causes the middleware to reject a mismatched origin preflight. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Token endpoints have no rate limiting — `backend/app/routers/tokens.py`'s `POST /tokens`, `GET /tokens`, and `POST /tokens/revoke` have no rate-limit dependency; `backend/app/routers/publish.py:25-29` defines `_limiter`/`get_rate_limiter()` but `tokens.py` never imports them. Before `require_wildcard_or_bootstrap` fully closes the bootstrap window, an unauthenticated caller can hammer `POST /tokens` at unlimited speed. Wire `Depends(get_rate_limiter)` (or a shared equivalent) onto at least `POST /tokens`, and add a test asserting 429 after the configured limit is exceeded on token issuance. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `MessageStore.load()` fetches all rows then slices in Python instead of using SQL LIMIT — `backend/app/persistence.py:121-127`: `select(MessageRecord).where(topic==topic).order_by(created_at)` returns every row; `items[-limit:]` runs in Python. For a `limit=1` call on a topic with 100 retained messages, 100 rows are fetched to return 1. Fix: chain `.order_by(MessageRecord.created_at.desc()).limit(limit)` when `limit` is set and reverse the result (`[::-1]`) for oldest-first output; handle `limit=None` without a LIMIT clause; add a test asserting the correct rows are returned. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `_limiter` in `publish.py` is frozen at module import time — `backend/app/routers/publish.py:25`: `_limiter = RateLimiter(get_settings().rate_limit_max, get_settings().rate_limit_window_seconds)` is evaluated once when the module is first imported, capturing `NOTIFY_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`/`NOTIFY_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW` at that instant. `backend/app/config.py`'s `Settings` docstring explicitly warns this pattern "silently ignores any env change afterward — the exact bug this class exists to prevent." Tests overriding `NOTIFY_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` after import and gunicorn prefork workers both see stale values. Fix: initialize `_limiter` lazily inside `get_rate_limiter()` using `Depends(get_settings)`, or move initialization into `startup_check()`; add a test that env override after import takes effect without reimporting. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-17 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 5 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] ⚠️ CORRECTION: invalid NOTIFY_QUIET_HOURS_TZ still 500s every publish — `broker.py:142` calls `should_deliver_now(message, self.quiet_hours_config)` without `tz=tz`; lines 137-140 compute a UTC fallback `tz` then never pass it, so `should_deliver_now()` re-raises `ValueError` for invalid TZ strings and every publish to a quiet-hours topic becomes a 500. `config.py::_load_quiet_hours():53` stores the raw unvalidated string despite `safe_resolve_timezone()` existing. - [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Drop slow subscribers cleanly instead of silently losing messages forever — `backend/app/services/broker.py::Broker.publish` counts `asyncio.QueueFull` per message but a permanently stuck subscriber queue stays registered and keeps dropping. Track consecutive QueueFull per subscriber queue, add a `Settings` field `slow_subscriber_max_drops` (env `NOTIFY_SLOW_SUBSCRIBER_MAX_DROPS`, default 20) in `backend/app/config.py`, unsubscribe a queue once it exceeds it, and make the SSE/WS loops in `backend/app/routers/subscribe.py` end when their queue has been dropped. ## Seeded round 7 2026-11-06 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `_env_float` accepts `nan` and `inf`, so one bad env value silently breaks severity gating or the rate limiter forever - `backend/app/config.py::_env_float` does `float(raw)` then only checks `value < minimum`, and every comparison with nan is False so nan passes (and inf passes the minimum check); verified by running with `NOTIFY_SEVERITY_GATE_THRESHOLD=nan`, `NOTIFY_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW=inf`, `NOTIFY_TTL_SECONDS=nan`: `Settings()` returns nan/inf unchanged, `Broker.is_actionable` then returns False for EVERY message that carries a severity (`level >= nan` is False, so critical alerts are suppressed and never stored), and `RateLimiter(60, inf)` has `refill_per_second == 0.0` so a topic that sends 60 messages is rate-limited forever (idle eviction `now - updated >= inf` never fires). Fix: in `_env_float` after parsing add `if not math.isfinite(value):` log the same style of warning and return `default` (add `import math`); leave `_env_int` alone. Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_config_env_float_nonfinite.py`: parametrize `nan`, `NaN`, `inf`, `-inf`, `Infinity` -> `_env_float('X', 7.5) == 7.5` with a caplog warning; `'2.5'` still -> 2.5; `Settings().severity_gate_threshold == 0.0` and `Settings().rate_limit_window_seconds == 60.0` when the env vars are nan/inf (red before: they return nan/inf). - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=true` (or `True`, `yes`, `on`) silently leaves the service OPEN with no auth, and `NOTIFY_PERSIST=true` silently leaves persistence off - `backend/app/config.py::Settings.require_auth` and `.persist` are `os.getenv(..., "0") == "1"`, so any truthy spelling other than exactly `1` is treated as false with no warning (fail-open); verified by running: with `NOTIFY_REQUIRE_AUTH=true NOTIFY_PERSIST=true`, `get_settings()` gives `require_auth False, persist False` (only `1` gives True), and `startup_check` therefore also skips the insecure-secret refusal. Fix: add `_env_flag(name: str, default: bool = False) -> bool` to `config.py` (unset -> default; stripped lower-case in `{'1','true','yes','on'}` -> True; in `{'0','false','no','off',''}` -> False; anything else -> log a warning and return default) and use it ONLY for `require_auth` and `persist` (leave `allow_token_bootstrap` and `allow_insecure_secret` strict-`1` on purpose: they widen access). Do NOT edit README (the README env table is owned by another item; `1` keeps working). Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_config_env_flag.py`: `true/True/YES/on/1` -> True, `0/false/off/` (empty) -> False, unset -> default, `treu` -> default plus a caplog warning, and `Settings().require_auth` / `.persist` follow it (red before: `true` -> False). - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] A JSON body containing `NaN`/`Infinity` or a lone UTF-16 surrogate makes `POST /{topic}` return a bare 500 instead of a 422 - `backend/app/main.py::request_validation_handler` delegates to FastAPI's default handler, whose 422 body echoes each error's `input` and `ctx`; verified by running with TestClient (`raise_server_exceptions=False`): `{"body":"a","priority":NaN}` -> 500 (`ValueError: Out of range float values are not JSON compliant: nan` when `JSONResponse` renders), and `{"body":"\ud800 x"}` / `{"body":"a","title":"\udfff"}` -> 500 (`UnicodeEncodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\ud800'` in `JSONResponse.render`), while `{"body":5}` correctly gives 422. Python's `json` accepts both spellings so any client can trigger it and the 500s are logged as server errors. Fix: in `request_validation_handler`, wrap the `await default_request_validation_handler(request, exc)` call in `try/except (ValueError, UnicodeEncodeError)` and on failure return `JSONResponse(status_code=422, content={'detail': [{k: v for k, v in e.items() if k not in ('input', 'ctx')} for e in exc.errors()]})` (the normal path and the NUL-byte 400 stay exactly as they are). Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_validation_error_unencodable_input.py` using `TestClient(app, raise_server_exceptions=False)` and `content=` raw bytes with `content-type: application/json`: NaN priority -> 422 with a `detail` list whose items have `loc`/`msg`/`type` and no `input`; Infinity body field -> 422; lone-surrogate body and title -> 422; a plain `{"body":5}` still 422 and still contains `input`; NUL byte still 400 (red before: three of these are 500). VERIFY: `cd backend && .venv/bin/pytest -q --no-cov tests/test_validation_error_unencodable_input.py tests/test_publish_validate_topic.py tests/test_schemas.py`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] A single publish with a lone-surrogate tag poisons the whole topic: `GET /{topic}/messages` returns 500 for every caller until the message ages out - `backend/app/schemas.py::PublishRequest.validate_tags` never checks that tags are encodable, and unlike `body`/`title` (which pydantic's `str` validation rejects) the tag list slips through; verified by running: `POST /s2 {"tags":["\ud800"],"body":"a"}` -> 200, `broker.history('s2')` holds `[['\ud800']]`, then `GET /s2/messages` raises `UnicodeEncodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\ud800'` (500) and `GET /s2/stats`, SSE replay and WS replay hit the same stored message. Fix: in `validate_tags` (the `mode="before"` validator) add a helper `_reject_surrogates(tag)` that does `tag.encode('utf-8')` inside `try/except UnicodeEncodeError` and raises `ValueError('tags must be valid UTF-8 text (lone surrogates are not allowed)')`; call it for every final tag in all three input shapes (list, comma string, JSON-array string). Do not touch `_reject_nul`. Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_schemas_surrogate_tags.py` at model level (no HTTP, so it does not depend on the 422-encoding fix): `PublishRequest(body='x', tags=['\ud800'])`, `tags='\ud800,ok'` and `tags='["\\ud800"]'` each raise pydantic `ValidationError`; `tags=['\U0001F600', 'caf\u00e9']` (valid astral/accented) still pass and round-trip (red before: the surrogate cases are accepted). VERIFY: `cd backend && .venv/bin/pytest -q --no-cov tests/test_schemas_surrogate_tags.py tests/test_schemas_tags.py tests/test_schemas.py`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `notify_cli.py watch` dies after 5 seconds of silence with an empty error message, so it cannot follow a quiet topic - `backend/scripts/notify_cli.py::main` runs `async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:` for the `watch` command, and httpx's default `Timeout(timeout=5.0)` applies to each read, while the server only sends a keep-alive comment every `KEEP_ALIVE_INTERVAL = 25` s; verified by running `watch_topic` against a local asyncio server that sends SSE headers then stays quiet for 8 s: it prints `Error watching topic: ` (the `httpx.ReadTimeout` has an empty `str()`) and `sys.exit(1)` after exactly 5.0 s. Fix: add `def make_watch_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient: return httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(10.0, read=None))` and use it in the `watch` branch of `main`; change the stderr line in `watch_topic` to `f"Error watching topic: {type(e).__name__}: {e}"`. Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_notify_cli_watch_timeout.py`: `make_watch_client().timeout.read is None` and `.timeout.connect == 10.0`; with `fetch_sse_stream` monkeypatched to raise `httpx.ReadTimeout('')`, `watch_topic` exits 1 and capsys stderr contains `ReadTimeout` (red before: no helper, and the message is empty). - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Tags `null` and nested values are silently turned into the strings `"None"`, `"['x']"` and `"{'k': 1}"` - `backend/app/schemas.py::PublishRequest.validate_tags` builds the list with `[str(t).strip() for t in v if str(t).strip()]` (and the same for decoded JSON), so any non-string element is stringified by Python repr; verified by running: `POST /topx1 {"body":"a","tags":["a",null]}` -> 200 stored tags `['a','None']`, `"tags":"[null]"` -> `['None']`, `[["x"],{"k":1}]` -> `["['x']","{'k': 1}"]`, `[1,true]` -> `['1','True']`. Numbers are intentionally coerced (existing `test_schemas_tags.py` sends `[1, 2, 'three']`), but None, bool, list and dict elements are always client mistakes. Fix: in `validate_tags`, before stringifying (list branch and decoded-JSON branch) raise `ValueError('each tag must be a string or number')` when an element is `None`, a `bool`, a `list` or a `dict`; keep int/float/str behaviour. Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_schemas_tags_nonstring.py`: `PublishRequest(body='x', tags=['a', None])`, `tags=[True]`, `tags=[['x']]`, `tags=[{'k': 1}]` and `tags='[null]'` raise `ValidationError`; `tags=[1, 2.5, 'three']` still gives `['1','2.5','three']` (red before: the first five are accepted). - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `publish_with_retry` sleeps for `nan` seconds when the server sends `Retry-After: nan` - `backend/app/utils/publish_client.py::publish_with_retry` does `ra_val = float(retry_after_header)` and `min(max(ra_val, backoff_delay(...)), max_retry_after)`; `float('nan')` parses and `max`/`min` with nan return nan, so `asyncio.sleep(nan)` is called (undefined scheduling: may return instantly, defeating the backoff, or never wake); verified by running against `httpx.MockTransport` returning 429 + `Retry-After: nan` then 200: the logged delay is `nans` and the recorded sleep argument is `nan`, while `-5`, `0` give 0.5 and `inf` gives 60.0. Fix: treat a non-finite `ra_val` like an unparseable header (`if not math.isfinite(ra_val): raise ValueError` inside the existing `try`, so it falls back to `backoff_delay(attempt, base=backoff_base)`); add `import math`. Tests in new file `backend/tests/test_publish_client_retry_after_nonfinite.py` using `httpx.MockTransport` and a module-scoped sleep recorder (`monkeypatch.setattr(publish_client, 'asyncio', types.SimpleNamespace(sleep=fake_sleep))`, never patch the global asyncio module): `Retry-After: nan` -> recorded sleeps `[0.5]` and final status 200; `inf` -> `[60.0]`; `-5` -> `[0.5]`; `abc` -> `[0.5]` (red before: nan case records nan). - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `cleanup_garbage.find_garbage_paths` skips `.github` (and any directory with `.git`/`venv` anywhere in its path) and still walks the entire `.git` and `.venv` trees - `backend/scripts/cleanup_garbage.py::find_garbage_paths` does `if '.git' in root or 'venv' in root: continue`, a substring test on the path that only skips processing, it never prunes `dirs`, so os.walk descends into every `.git`/`.venv` file, while `.github`, `.gitlab`, `.gitkeep-dir` and e.g. `prevent/` are treated as skipped; verified by running in a temp tree containing `.github/Wait, looking x.txt`, `x/Let's look y.txt` and `.venv/lib/Let's look z.txt`: only `./x/Let's look y.txt` is reported (the `.github` artifact is missed, which is exactly the leak class the pre-commit hook exists for). Fix: define `SKIP_DIRS = {'.git', '.venv', 'venv', 'node_modules', '__pycache__'}`, prune with `dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in SKIP_DIRS]` before the garbage-dir loop, and delete the substring `continue`. - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `NOTIFY_DATABASE_URL` in the form hosting providers hand out crashes startup with a confusing driver error - `backend/app/config.py::Settings.database_url` is passed verbatim to `create_async_engine` (`MessageStore.__init__`, outside the try block in `main.startup_check`), and SQLAlchemy rejects the common spellings; verified by running `create_async_engine`: `postgres://u:p@h/db` -> `NoSuchModuleError: Can't load plugin: sqlalchemy.dialects:postgres`, `postgresql://u:p@h/db` -> `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'psycopg2'` (sync driver, not in requirements), `sqlite:///x.db` -> `InvalidRequestError: The asyncio extension requires an async driver`; only `postgresql+asyncpg://` and `sqlite+aiosqlite://` work although `requirements.txt` pins exactly `asyncpg` and `aiosqlite`. - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `backoff_delay`/`retry_delay` raise OverflowError for a large attempt number instead of returning the cap - `backend/app/utils/backoff.py`: both compute `min(base * (2 ** max(attempt, 0)), cap)`; verified `backoff_delay(2000)` and `retry_delay(2000)` raise `OverflowError: int too large to convert to float` (publish_with_retry feeds `attempt` into it, and a caller passing a large `max_retries` would crash mid-retry instead of sleeping the cap). Fix: clamp the exponent first, e.g. `exp = min(max(attempt, 0), 60)` in both functions (2**60 * any sane base already exceeds the cap). Add to the existing `backend/tests/test_backoff.py`: `test_backoff_delay_huge_attempt_returns_cap` asserting `backoff_delay(2000) == 30.0` and `backoff_delay(10**6, cap=5.0) == 5.0`, and `test_retry_delay_huge_attempt_returns_cap` asserting `retry_delay(2000) == 60.0`; keep the existing assertions (attempt 0/1/2/-1/10) passing. VERIFY: `cd backend && .venv/bin/pytest -q --no-cov tests/test_backoff.py` and confirm the two new huge-attempt tests fail on the old code with OverflowError and pass after, with the old tests still green. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `QuietHoursManager(max_held=0)` crashes with IndexError on the first `hold()`, and `is_within_quiet_hours` ignores `config.timezone` for naive datetimes - `backend/app/utils/quiet_hours.py`: (1) `hold()` does `if len(self._held_messages) >= self._max_held: self._held_messages.pop(0)` which on an empty list raises `IndexError: pop from empty list` for `max_held=0` (verified); fix: when `self._max_held <= 0` just count `self._overflow_count += 1` and return without appending. (2) `is_within_quiet_hours` treats a naive dt as UTC but then skips the `astimezone(resolve_timezone(config.timezone))` conversion, so `is_within_quiet_hours(datetime(2026, 1, 1, 12, 0), QuietHoursConfig(20, 23, "Asia/Tokyo"))` returns False although 12:00 UTC is 21:00 in Tokyo (verified False; `should_deliver_now` and the aware-datetime branch do convert); fix: `local_dt = dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc) if dt.tzinfo is None else dt`, then always `.astimezone(tz)`. Extend `backend/tests/test_quiet_hours.py`: `test_manager_max_held_zero_counts_overflow_without_error` (hold two messages, no exception, `_held_messages == []`, `_overflow_count == 2`) and `test_naive_datetime_converted_to_config_timezone` (the Tokyo case above is True; the same naive dt with the default `"UTC"` config and window 20-23 is False). VERIFY: `cd backend && .venv/bin/pytest -q --no-cov tests/test_quiet_hours.py` and confirm the two new tests fail before and the existing naive-datetime tests (UTC config) still pass. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] A deploy/restart waits forever on open SSE/WebSocket connections because uvicorn has no graceful-shutdown deadline - `backend/start.sh` ends with `exec uvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port "${PORT:-8000}"`, and subscribers hold `GET /{topic}/sse` streams open indefinitely, so on SIGTERM uvicorn waits for them until the platform SIGKILLs it (uvicorn 0.30 supports `--timeout-graceful-shutdown`). Fix: append `--timeout-graceful-shutdown 10` to that uvicorn command line (keep `exec`, host and port unchanged). Extend `backend/tests/test_start_sh.py` with `test_start_sh_sets_graceful_shutdown_timeout` that reads `start.sh` text and asserts `"--timeout-graceful-shutdown 10" in text` and that the text still contains `exec uvicorn app.main:app`.
